POSTGRESQL 18 · LOCAL SOCKET ONLY · SYNTHETIC DATA

Link model bake-off

Choose typed links when domain existence must hold for every SQL writer.

A enforces links to domain rows and rejects cross-kind UUID mixups. B accepts registry-only targets and domain deletion behind live edges. C accepts orphan targets and cross-kind UUID mixups. B's measured context p50 is 0.134 ms versus A's 0.205 ms; p95 is 1.589 versus 1.964 ms. The default is conditional on requiring domain-row existence for every database writer.

The verdict flips to a registry when reverse domain existence and deletion cleanup are enforced in the database, every legacy key has a collision-safe mapping, and measured context-read savings or frequent kind additions outweigh registry lifecycle and migration costs. The registry specified here fails the reverse-existence tests. Same-transaction insertion fixes crash drift but does not fix committed bypasses.

Raw resultsC → A SQLC → B SQL

Filter measurement rows or expand source evidence below.

Dataset

Assumed planning baseline, not a production census. Core sizes model a growing brokerage/knowledge system; auxiliary kinds assume 100 rows each. Full run multiplies every kind and each 3,000-edge family by ten. Parties represent contacts/persons and companies/orgs. Synthetic UUIDs are globally distinct across kinds; text IDs and digests are normalized solely for this identity-layer experiment.

Integrity

Raw SQL storage probes, rolled back independently. Undefined typed target columns reject structurally (42703), not by FK. Baseline preserves fixed-source FK and selected kind/uniqueness checks; no target-existence FK. Baseline is an identity projection, not a replay of envelope/permission logic.

Depth-two context latency

Bidirectional, distinct vertices at minimum depth, includes root, depth <= 2. Full fetch and sort timed over Unix socket; warm cache; no result cap. 1000 identical uniformly sampled refs per run; 5 interleaved repetitions. Results compared by hash for every query. Median result vertices 38.0; maximum 4584.

Designp50 msp95 msp50 range across runsErrors
A0.2051.964[0.196, 0.25]0
B0.1341.589[0.13, 0.164]0
C0.2071.9[0.199, 0.25]0

Domain row plus three links write cost

Each operation commits with fsync and synchronous_commit enabled. Update changes payload and all three target IDs; delete removes links then domain and registry. SQL round trips and constraint checks are timed; no batching advantage for a side. All successful writes commit and are later verified absent after deletion.

Eight concurrent writers

Independent cycle = insert + update + delete transactions. Hotspot = same domain row and its links, with a deliberate 1ms hold to force contention. pg_stat_activity samples measure observed lock occupancy, not exact wait time. Thread setup is included in throughput. Full raw repetition data is in JSON.

Registry drift and killed transactions

pg_terminate_backend kills only a backend in the owned temporary cluster. Same-transaction uncommitted rows disappear. A committed registry-only row survives without a domain row. Integrity tests also show domain deletion can leave B edges pointing at a registry tombstone.

DesignMidpointReadback
Aafter registry or first domain write{"domain_rows_after_kill": 0, "registry_rows_after_kill": null, "drift": false}
Aafter domain before links{"domain_rows_after_kill": 0, "registry_rows_after_kill": null, "drift": false}
Bafter registry or first domain write{"domain_rows_after_kill": 0, "registry_rows_after_kill": 0, "drift": false}
Bafter domain before links{"domain_rows_after_kill": 0, "registry_rows_after_kill": 0, "drift": false}

Cafter domain before links{"domain_rows_after_kill": 0, "registry_rows_after_kill": null, "drift": false}

Online migration rehearsal

SQL files alongside this report contain executable shadow setup, transactional capture, repeatable-read backfill, final touched-row reconciliation under source locks, read-view cutover, and synchronous dual-write. Statements count client-submitted SQL including transaction control; scans count every UNION arm. Cutover uses bounded NOWAIT retries that release partial locks. Equality checks run after the writer stops. This proves continued writes with bounded queuing, not zero write latency. Existing invalid data, identifier mappings, grants, and app writer cutover remain deployment work; this does not authorize production migration.

TargetSQL statementsBackfill passesBackfill sCDC changesWriter cycles / errorsCutover attempt + lock msWriter max cycle msMatch after dual-write

B145753.3834392372 / 076.1279.81True

Method and limits

Five repetitions per side, interleaved reference order and shuffled write-side order; one run only in selftest mode. End-to-end SQL fetch/commit timing includes local client overhead. Identical data, indexes in both directions, statistics refreshed, warm cache, fsync on. EXPLAIN separates server execution and planning and exposes index probes plus recursive result aggregation. Graph equality is checked for every reference and all successful write cycles are verified removed. Concurrent hotspot holds intentionally add 1ms; throughput is for the specified workload on this host, not a production capacity estimate. Baseline C preserves identity-relevant constraints from the migration-defined families, including fixed-source FKs, selected kind checks and duplicate checks; metadata, envelopes, per-owner uniqueness, source revision pins, and verb validation are excluded. A and B add explicit relationship uniqueness as a proposed contract. Open target vocabularies use the declared synthetic core set in A/B. Inventory includes broader pointer candidates and is distinct from the timed relationship families. No network database, production census, Neon command, credentials, or paid model call is used.

Tuning correction: the initial A design had a generated kind/ID read index but no indexes on its nullable FK columns. Adding 70 partial FK indexes changed median A delete throughput from 186.1 to 5112.7 transactions/s. All three sides were rerun after tuning. The untuned-results.json file is retained for diagnosis and is superseded.

Primary references: PostgreSQL 18 constraints, ALTER TABLE locking, Concurrent index builds.
